## Owner clarification — outer bench ring + real voxel terrain assets — 2026-09-20

**Status: OWNER APPROVED; supersedes earlier same-day wording that placed bench cells on/replaced cells in the wood ring.**

- **Bench topology:** the wood ring is always its own complete inner perimeter. A connected wooden fence sits above those wood blocks. Bench/waiting cells are a **separate contiguous block ring one full block farther outside**; no bench cell replaces or skips a wood block.
- **Historical bench reference:** the newly supplied `old_src.zip` was read as reference only. `old_src/scenes/shared/planningBenchRuntime.ts::createPlanningBenchSlots()` creates slot UI/pagination and delegates click behavior to `onPlanningBenchClick`; `old_src/scenes/shared/planningFormationRuntime.ts` delegates board↔bench / bench↔bench mutations to BoardSystem/BoardBenchOps and derives the cap from bench upgrade/bonus state. The archive remains protected and is not an implementation target. Its former direct `upgradePlanningBench()` hard-coded 10-gold mutation is historical presentation-era behavior and is **not** copied over the current Tech Tree authority.
- **Real voxel grass authority:** `public/assets/environment/voxel-terrain/source-manifest.json` identifies `Voxel_Animal_Asset_Pack/Main/Green + Main/Pink`; `src/three/terrain/ThreeVoxelTerrainData.ts` is generated from the supplied OBJ + MagicaVoxel palette. The current `ThreeVoxelTerrain.getVoxelTerrainGeometry()` replaces those source meshes with `BoxGeometry` and `blockColorFor()`; that procedural substitution is a defect for this owner request. Runtime terrain must consume the source asset geometry/vertex colors.
- **Grass/soil composition:** top terrain blocks use the supplied `grass1/grass2` source meshes; buried hill/foundation blocks use supplied `nograss`. Source vertex colors are preserved so the grass top and dirt/soil sides come from the asset rather than a hand-authored light/dark-green material.
- **Face elimination:** each visual block remains one class/object. Neighbor-hidden cube boundary triangles must be omitted from the generated BufferGeometry; this is source-asset face filtering, not replacing the source mesh with a procedural cube.
- **Stone bench asset audit:** recursive current-tree + supplied source snapshot + voxel-pack intake scan finds **no dedicated stone / rock / cobble terrain block asset**. `green_walktile` exists but is a gold/yellow path tile, not a stone asset, so it must not be falsely relabeled as stone. Bench placement/locking may be implemented with the existing walktile as a clearly marked temporary source-asset fallback, but final stone appearance remains **ASSET PENDING** until the owner supplies a stone/cobble voxel source.
- **Clickable locks:** inactive bench cells remain visibly represented and carry a 3D lock hit target. A lock click may buy exactly one valid bench-capacity Tech Tree level through the current core authority; Three must not subtract gold or mutate `techLevels` directly.
- **Verification constraint:** source/static implementation only here. No build, browser, FPS, PWA, memory, or all-tests PASS claim. Full affected `tests/three` remains mandatory before an all-gates claim.

- **Runtime evidence:** source-level object metadata must expose grid coordinates, face mask and rendered-face count; internal neighbor faces must be absent from BufferGeometry rather than merely hidden behind depth testing.
- **Done criterion:** every terrain/soil/wood/water/bench cube is represented by a `ThreeVoxelBlock` object and geometry vertex/triangle count equals exposed faces only.

### Implementation record — face-culling / fence / waiting-cell tech unlock

- Added renderer-only `src/three/ThreeVoxelBlock.ts`. Every terrain/foundation/water/waiting cube is owned by one `ThreeVoxelBlock extends THREE.Object3D`; occupancy produces a six-direction face mask.
- For supplied voxel terrain assets, face culling **does not replace the asset with BoxGeometry**. `getFaceCulledVoxelSourceGeometry()` preserves source OBJ vertices and MagicaVoxel-derived vertex colors, while omitting only triangles that lie wholly on a hidden base-block boundary plane. Grass/path relief triangles that are not boundary planes remain present.
- Battlefield top blocks consume Green/Pink `grass1/grass2`; buried/foundation blocks consume Green `nograss`. Terrain remains on canonical 2.15 cell spacing and whole-block elevation.
- Water remains a renderer-only cube source, one `ThreeVoxelBlock` per river row, with shared interior faces omitted. Canonical river rejection/grid math is unchanged.
- The wood perimeter now keeps **all 36 snapped ring cells**. Bench cells no longer replace wood. A connected fence is generated above the complete ring with one post per ring cell and two rails per adjacent ring edge.
- Waiting cells live on a separate, contiguous perimeter exactly one full block outside the fence. Three owns a 32-cell presentation pool only as visual headroom; gameplay capacity still comes exclusively from `resolvePlanningBenchCap()`.
- Waiting-cell geometry uses the supplied `green_walktile` mesh as a provenance-marked temporary fallback because the audited source pack contains no stone/cobble block. `public/assets/environment/voxel-terrain/source-manifest.json` records `stoneAssetPending`.
- Inactive preview cells carry a 3D lock + transparent raycast hit target. The Planning controller never subtracts gold or edits `techLevels`: it delegates one purchase to new renderer-neutral `src/core/benchTechUnlock.ts`, which selects `explore → bench_up → barracks` from `TECH_BY_ID` and calls canonical `researchPlayerTech()`.
- After a successful lock purchase, Planning recomputes the canonical bench cap, refreshes lock visibility, units/HUD/tech UI, audio and persistence. Normal bench moves remain compact-array/cap-gated through the existing shared BoardBenchOps contract, matching the reference semantics observed in `old_src.zip`.
- Targeted source tests were added/updated for asset-triangle culling, block ownership metadata, full fenced ring adjacency, outer waiting-cell topology, Tech Tree delegation, Planning lock purchase flow, river face culling and voxel provenance.
- **Known pre-existing hard-rule defect remains open:** `src/scenes/ThreeDemoScene.ts` reverse-imports `src/three/**`. It is not deleted/moved in this pass because the repository hard rule requires regenerated inventory/closure plus test/typecheck/build/browser evidence before legacy live-source deletion. This pass introduces no new `src/scenes/** → src/three/**` edge and no Three→Phaser runtime import.

## Runtime closure cleanup + Minecraft block normalization — 2026-09-20

**Status: SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; SAFE GARBAGE CLEANUP APPLIED; full affected Three verification pending.**

Owner instruction: scan the full repository, remove tracked garbage, preserve `old_src/` without exception, keep the live Three runtime intact, and normalize arena terrain/wood/water/bench presentation to integer Minecraft-like cubes.

### Full-repository audit

- GitHub HEAD audited: `ea52be9d07c44a99b9c2c5fa3a50304713d192f8`.
- Recursive tracked tree: **2,475 files**.
- Uploaded source snapshot independently contains the same **2,475 tracked files**.
- Default Three entry analysis from `src/main.ts`, excluding only the explicit legacy `initGame()` Phaser rollback dynamic-import arm: **361 / 409 source modules reachable**, **52 source modules outside that default runtime closure**.
- The closure is **not yet transitively Phaser-free**: several canonical/shared modules reached by Three still contain Phaser imports (some type-only and some runtime). Therefore “outside the static closure” is not by itself a deletion authorization.
- Immediate safe-deletion set contains **23 tracked artifacts** with no runtime authority: `.vitest-ls.json-shm`, `.vitest-ls.json-wal`, `test-results/.last-run.json`, eight `CombatScene.ts.atomic-bak*` files and twelve `CombatScene.ts.hand-bak*` files.
- `old_src/` is protected. It must never be deleted or rewritten by this cleanup, even when absent from the GitHub archive because it is gitignored.
- The remaining 52 non-default-runtime source files are classified as **deferred**, not garbage, until their test/barrel/rollback/import consumers are rewritten or removed with a fresh closure proof.

### Block-layout implementation contract

- Define one visual block unit as `BLOCK_SIZE = TILE_SIZE + TILE_SPACING = 2.15` world units.
- Every terrain, wood, water and bench block is a true cube: **2.15 × 2.15 × 2.15**. No 0.08/0.16/0.25/0.82 “almost-level” terrain pieces are allowed for those block classes.
- All normal arena top faces share one exact integer block level. Hills are made only by stacking whole cubes at integer block levels; no fractional terrain elevation.
- Keep canonical gameplay coordinates/raycast surfaces unchanged. Cube presentation extends downward from the canonical board surface rather than moving unit placement Y.
- The combat field remains **11 visual columns × 5 rows**: 5 ally + 1 river + 5 enemy, while gameplay remains 10 logical playable columns.
- River is rendered as one exact column of full water cubes. Decorative flow/lily detail may sit on the water surface but cannot change water-block dimensions.
- Replace stretched wood beams with a **one-block-thick cube ring** around the 11×5 field.
- Bench lives **on that outer wood ring**, not one clearance outside it. Bench block centers snap to ring-cell centers. Adjacent ring cells touch face-to-face with zero world-space gap.
- Canonical bench indices/cap remain unchanged. Unlocked bench cells remain raycast/placeable; locked cells stay visually present as wood bench blocks with 3D locks but reject placement.
- Remove duplicate bench frame/terrain meshes so a bench cell owns exactly one cube body and cannot z-fight/overlap with the wood ring.
- Camera framing must use the snapped outer ring footprint.

### Implementation record

- Arena block authority is now `ARENA_BLOCK_SIZE = 2.15`. Foundation, terrain, wood, water and bench cube bodies all use **2.15 × 2.15 × 2.15** geometry.
- Canonical playable tile hit meshes remain thin and transparent; cube presentation extends downward from the unchanged canonical surface Y=0.5. Unit placement/raycast coordinates are therefore not raised by the Minecraft presentation.
- Hill elevation is quantized to whole block levels only: 0 / 2.15 / 4.30 / 6.45. Fractional 0.08/0.16/0.72 terrain offsets were removed.
- The river visual is now one full cube per row in the canonical river column. Fractional raised bank strips were removed; flow lines and lily pads remain decoration-only.
- Wood perimeter is now an exact one-cube-thick ring generated from snapped ring cells. Stretched beam geometry and sub-block lip/post heights were removed from the ring.
- Bench slots occupy consecutive cells **on the same wood ring**. The wood generator skips those exact cells and the bench presentation fills them with one wood cube, eliminating wood/bench overlap and world-space clearance gaps.
- Bench presentation no longer creates four extra frame beams plus a second terrain slab. Each bench slot owns one cube body plus an optional 3D lock.
- Camera framing consumes the snapped ring footprint and block size.
- `.gitignore` now blocks Vitest sqlite sidecars, `test-results/`, and accidental `*.hand-bak*` / `*.atomic-bak*` source backups.
- The 23 tracked garbage artifacts identified by the audit are deleted in this implementation commit.
- The **52 source modules outside the measured default Three closure are not blindly deleted**. They remain deferred because some are rollback/test/tool authorities or have live consumers. This preserves the user-mandated `old_src/` protection and avoids converting a cleanup request into an unverified rules-engine deletion.

Post-implementation HEAD `3b0872f7b4304e671badfaf19f1de67960f23d84` was read back from GitHub. Confirmed in committed source: `ARENA_BLOCK_SIZE = TILE_SIZE + TILE_SPACING`; bench cells use consecutive ring cells; decorative wood skips the same canonical bench cells; hill step equals one full block; bench frame beam duplication is removed; river uses `battlefield-river-water-blocks` with one instanced cube per row and no left/right bank strips; camera framing includes ring span; all 23 audited garbage paths are absent from the recursive tree. Tracked file count dropped from **2,475 → 2,452**. `old_src/` was not touched.

Verification limitation: the uploaded archive contains no `node_modules`, and package-manager registry access is unavailable in this environment, so Vitest/typecheck could not be executed locally. Per `AGENTS.md`, no all-gates PASS is claimed.

## Illustrated 11×5 arena replacement — 2026-09-20

**Status: SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; verification pending.**

The approved visual sketch is now treated as the arena presentation spec: **11 visual columns × 5 rows = 5 ally logical columns + 1 non-playable river column + 5 enemy logical columns**. Canonical combat state remains 10 logical columns / 50 playable cells; the river remains the existing canonical gap rather than becoming a new playable cell.

Implementation decisions:
- New procedural asset module `ThreeBattlefieldArenaAssets.ts` owns the presentation footprint, thick earth foundation, one continuous wood ring, four corner posts/lanterns, and the clockwise bench-perimeter path.
- Bench index remains canonical. `benchToWorld()` is the single world-position source for bench tile, unit avatar and drag projection, but its path now wraps outside the wood ring instead of forming one straight front row.
- Voxel outer terrain receives a thick soil body below every top tile plus deterministic stepped hill clusters at the arena outskirts. This is presentation-only and never changes board/unit gameplay Y.
- `grass2` geometry receives a deterministic dark vertex-color grade so the light/dark grass checker is visibly stronger without adding texture/network assets.
- River presentation keeps the canonical 2.15-wide rejection corridor while adding flow markers, lily pads and a frame-driven animation hook.
- Camera framing uses the full 11-column visual footprint plus bench ring and viewport aspect, so the new perimeter remains frameable on portrait and landscape.
- All replacement assets are generated from Three.js geometry/materials or the already-integrated voxel source data; there is no new runtime image dependency.

**Acceptance:** wood ring encloses only the 11×N visual battlefield; bench sits outside that ring on all four sides; combat remains 10 logical playable columns; visible earth blocks are thick; outer hills form stepped voxel clusters; checker contrast is stronger; river has animated visual detail; camera includes the full perimeter.

No build, smoke, Playwright, or all-gates PASS is claimed by this source implementation.

## Visual terrain / camera / i18n correction plan — 2026-09-20

**Status: SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; targeted/full affected Three verification pending.**

**User evidence:** the latest Planning and result-modal screenshots show four concrete defects: (1) raw i18n keys such as `ui.retry` and `menu.mainMenu` leak into the result modal, (2) terrain presentation leaves visible seams/gaps between adjacent grass tiles instead of a continuous ground plane, (3) the central river is still rendered as a flat strip rather than a proper 3D river tile/bank treatment, and (4) the tactical camera is not user-pannable from an on-screen mobile control.

**Brainstormed implementation decisions (source-of-truth constrained):**

- **I18n repair:** audit the result overlay and any shared button-label helpers for raw key assignment. All visible labels must flow through the existing Three i18n function and the active locale dictionaries. Acceptance: no literal `ui.*`, `menu.*`, or `three.*` key is visible in the result modal for VI/EN; `ui.retry` and `menu.mainMenu` are explicitly covered by tests.
- **No-gap terrain presentation:** canonical board math remains `TILE_SIZE=2.0`, `TILE_SPACING=0.15`, step `2.15`, and the river gap remains canonical/non-playable. The presentation mesh must visually fill the full 2.15-cell footprint instead of shrinking to ~0.96, so adjacent visual terrain touches while canonical tile centers/hitboxes remain unchanged. Do **not** mutate core board spacing to solve a visual seam.
- **Checkerboard grass:** battlefield/environment ground uses deterministic light/dark grass alternation based on `(row + col) % 2`; occasional dirt/scenery may remain only where it does not break the readable checker pattern. No `Math.random()` in terrain layout.
- **3D height variation:** use the uploaded voxel terrain geometry as actual block presentation. Outer terrain gets deterministic low-amplitude elevation bands (presentation-only) so blocks read as 3D/uneven; canonical board placement/raycast surfaces stay flat and unchanged. Units must never inherit terrain height as gameplay state.
- **River tile treatment:** keep `ThreeRiverDivider` as the canonical non-playable river authority (width 2.15, centered between logical columns 4 and 5), but replace the visually flat strip with a shallow 3D water channel plus voxel-like bank/lip geometry. River remains non-raycastable/non-placeable and profile-aware for SOLO/COOP depth.
- **Camera movement:** extend `ThreeCameraManager` with a bounded pan API that moves camera position and OrbitControls target together in board/world XZ, preserving current isometric orbit/zoom. Clamp panning to the visible terrain footprint so the player cannot lose the board.
- **On-screen joystick:** add a persistent left-side DOM joystick owned by `ThreeApp` (not by Planning/Combat controller) so it survives Planning ↔ Combat scene replacement. Fixed base, movable thumb, normalized x/y output, pointer capture, safe-area aware, and no overlap with the bottom action/shop dock. Joystick input pans the camera only; it must not affect unit drag/drop or gameplay state.
- **Desktop parity:** keyboard camera pan (WASD/arrow keys) may share the same bounded pan API; OrbitControls remains available for mouse/touch orbit/zoom. The joystick is presentation/input only and can be hidden or reduced on pointer-fine desktop if desired.
- **Lifecycle:** joystick listeners/DOM and camera input state must be disposed with `ThreeApp`. Any new shared-runtime method added to `ThreeCameraManager` requires updating all test doubles/mocks per `AGENTS.md`.
- **Verification scope:** targeted tests must cover i18n result labels, river presentation invariants, no-gap/checker/elevation terrain rules, camera pan/clamp, joystick pointer behavior, and lifecycle. Because `ThreeCameraManager`/`ThreeApp` are shared runtime, the full affected `tests/three` directory remains mandatory before any all-gates claim. No build/smoke/Playwright is authorized by this request.

**Acceptance checklist:** no raw result-modal i18n keys; no visible black seams between adjacent terrain cells; obvious light/dark grass checker; shallow visible 3D river/channel; deterministic raised/lowered voxel ground outside the canonical battlefield; left-side joystick can pan camera in four directions and diagonals; camera remains clamped; Planning/Combat share the same joystick/camera state ownership; unit drag/click/placement remains canonical.

**Implementation record for this correction:** general VI/EN dictionaries now define `ui.retry` and `menu.mainMenu`; Planning's cortisol/settings labels and cortisol log are routed through i18n. Voxel terrain presentation uses exact cell-fill X/Z scale `(TILE_SIZE + TILE_SPACING) / TILE_SIZE = 1.075`, deterministic `grass1/grass2` checker selection, and deterministic outer elevation levels 0/0.08/0.16 without changing canonical board Y/raycast state. The river keeps canonical width/depth authority but now renders a shallow channel root plus water surface and two 3D banks. `ThreeCameraJoystick` is owned by `ThreeApp`, persists across round phase replacement, is shown only for Planning/Combat, pans camera + OrbitControls target together relative to view direction, and clamps target X/Z to ±24. **Desktop camera parity is now wired through the same input owner and pan function: held W/A/S/D (plus arrow keys) generate normalized 4-way/diagonal camera vectors, keyup/blur clears held state, editable INPUT/TEXTAREA/SELECT/contenteditable targets are ignored, and keyboard input is inactive outside Planning/Combat.** The implementation deliberately uses the already-public camera/controls surface instead of adding a new `ThreeCameraManager` method, avoiding a shared-runtime signature expansion and the associated mock churn. Targeted source tests cover WASD/arrow mapping, held-key camera movement, key release, editable-field protection, pan/clamp, joystick lifecycle and DOM disposal; no build/smoke/Playwright or all-tests PASS is claimed from this connector-only implementation.

Owner-approved status presentation is now implemented as one dynamic canvas-backed Three.js billboard shared by Planning and Combat:

- Top plaque shows the unit/monster name with PNG star icon.
- HP row shows remaining HP percentage in a left badge, a dynamic green/yellow/red HP bar in the middle, and exact `current / max` HP in the right badge.
- Rage row is **segmented/countable**, not a smooth fill. It renders one cell per rage point up to 10 visual cells, preserves exact `current / rageMax` text, and has visible inner/cell gaps. Rage costs above 10 keep exact numeric text while using 10 proportional visual cells.
- Status row groups positive buffs on the **left** with green frames and negative/debug/debuff effects on the **right** with red frames. Related canonical fields such as `atkBuffTurns` + `atkBuffValue` collapse to one tile.
- Combat calls `setStatusEffects(statuses, mods)` on spawn, canonical visual refresh, authoritative state application and post-action refresh, so status icons follow actual combat state.
- Planning board units use the same billboard renderer; Planning currently passes no combat statuses but gets the same name/HP/rage visual language.
- Status icons use the shared `src/core/emojiIcon.ts` semantic mapping and are drawn as Unicode emoji directly into the status canvas; optional emoji-api.com metadata can hydrate the canonical character when `VITE_EMOJI_API_KEY` is configured.

**Emoji/icon cleanup (supersedes the former PNG fallback):** `ThreeProductionUiAssets.createFreeIconOrGlyphElement()`, DOM iconization, `ThreeEmojiAtlas`, unit avatar fallback icons and combat star pips now resolve through the shared Unicode authority. No icon atlas fallback is loaded; unknown semantic names degrade to the local `❔` Unicode fallback while preserving gameplay/UI readability.

Targeted source tests cover buff/debuff grouping, duplicate-effect collapse, HP percentage, segmented rage counts, combat status billboard updates and asset-free Unicode icon behavior. No browser/build/smoke/Playwright or full `tests/three` PASS is claimed from this connector-only implementation.

## 0. Evidence and file policy

Every implementation case below records all seven mandatory fields:

- **Source symbol:** the existing Phaser/core symbol that defines behavior.
- **Migration method:** exact method (`M1`–`M4`) defining architectural interaction.
- **Allowed files:** files that may change for the case.
- **Forbidden files/dependencies:** files or imports that MUST remain untouched or prohibited.
- **Targeted Vitest:** deterministic test file or suite that proves the contract.
- **Runtime evidence:** browser, build, or device evidence required when static tests cannot prove behavior.
- **Done criterion:** observable, bounded, and measurable acceptance condition.

Global policy:

- Use `pnpm`; use `pnpm install`, `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm run build`, and targeted `pnpm exec vitest --run ...` commands.
- No `src/scenes/**` module may import `src/three/**`. This includes direct imports and re-export paths. Three hosts may consume shared/core code, never the reverse.
- No Three counterpart may own a duplicate shop pool, recipe table, synergy threshold, damage formula, AI rule, combat queue, persistence envelope, or network payload schema.
- Phaser scene adapters may use Phaser APIs. Renderer-neutral logic MUST use structural host interfaces and core/system data.
- `src/three/**` MUST remain free of runtime Phaser imports. Comments may describe the source contract, but comments are not runtime evidence.
- A static test may verify an import graph or artifact shape; it MUST NOT be used to claim that a browser route, PWA worker, FPS target, memory target, or touch interaction works.

Migration method vocabulary:
- **M1 — Direct consume:** Three host imports renderer-neutral constants/data/functions directly from `src/core`, `src/data`, or `src/systems` without copying rules.
- **M2 — Shared contract adapter:** Extract or expose a structural interface around a canonical runtime so Phaser and Three hosts call the same operation with host-specific rendering callbacks.
- **M3 — Pure extraction:** Move a renderer-neutral formula/state transition from a Phaser host into a shared module, migrate every caller, and retain Phaser rendering only in the host adapter.
- **M4 — Presentation adapter:** Keep canonical state/events authoritative and implement Three-only meshes, DOM, audio, VFX, input projection, or lifecycle behavior that cannot affect gameplay state.

### 0.3 Boot path invariants and deployment reality (2026-09-17)

#### 0.3.1 Static import graph purity (zero static Phaser on default boot)
- **Rule:** The static import graph rooted at `src/main.ts` (static `import` and `export ... from` declarations only, excluding dynamic `import()`) MUST contain **zero** path to the `phaser` package or Phaser scene / configuration files.
- **Rollback path (superseded 2026-09-22):** runtime Phaser boot was removed. Historical rollback authority is branch `archived/2D-Phaser`; production `src/main.ts` is Three-only.
- **Enforcement artifact:** `tests/three/threeBootStaticGraph.test.ts` (`collectStaticPhaserPaths() === []`). Every test run enforces that no static dependency edge leads to `phaser`.
- **Prohibition:** No newly added statically-imported module in the entry graph may transitively import `phaser`. Adding a static Phaser import to the default Three.js boot path is a blocking architectural violation.

#### 0.3.2 Fatal boot error boundary (no silent blank page)
- **Rule:** A boot failure MUST NEVER result in a silent blank page.
- **Implementation:** `src/main.ts` installs `window.addEventListener("error", ...)` and `window.addEventListener("unhandledrejection", ...)` before bootstrap begins, wraps `bootstrapApp()` in a `.catch(...)` handler, and renders a DOM-only fatal error panel into `#app` using localized copy (`three.boot.fatalTitle` and `three.boot.fatalHint` in both `src/i18n/vi.ts` and `src/i18n/en.ts`) without any runtime Three.js or Phaser dependency.
- **Residual silent-risk paths (acknowledged):**
  1. Errors originating inside un-awaited asynchronous callbacks scheduled *after* `ThreeApp` construction that do not bubble to window listeners.
  2. Non-DOM runtime environments where `document.getElementById("app")` is absent (the renderer no-ops safely).

#### 0.3.3 Deployment reality and operational hazards
- **Live origin architecture:** The public URL `https://forest-thorne.devgovietnam.io.vn/` is served directly by a persistent `vite dev --host --port 5173` process running in the same working tree on the workstation, exposed through a Cloudflare Tunnel. There is no automated build or deploy step; file edits in the workspace immediately alter the live site. A non-booting working tree constitutes an instant live outage.
- **`dist/` and deployment scripts:** The `dist/` production bundle is currently NOT served on the public origin (its hashed asset requests return 404 and fall back to `index.html`). The `deploy/` directory is currently empty, though `package.json` references `deploy/deploy-ssh.sh`.
- **Open User Decision (Not an engineering blocker):** *Whether to replace the live Vite dev server with a containerized/static production build serving `dist/`.* (Raised by lead; marked OPEN pending user decision).
- **Measured evidence (2026-09-17):** Full HTTP-level crawl of `https://forest-thorne.devgovietnam.io.vn/` (HTTP measurement only, not a browser render) fetched 289 local modules with 0 failures and 4 optimized-dep URLs:
  - `200` `/node_modules/.vite/deps/three.js?v=d622dd03`
  - `200` `/node_modules/.vite/deps/three_addons_controls_OrbitControls__js.js?v=7b9d1252`
  - `200` `/node_modules/.vite/deps/@discord_embedded-app-sdk.js?v=16330a13`
  - `504` `/node_modules/.vite/deps/phaser.js?v=3f5a34cd` (content-length: 16, body `error code: 504`) — the former Phaser rollback route was broken live at that historical point; that route was removed on 2026-09-22.
- **Two distinct hazards stacked:**
  1. *Hazard 1 (Source-level static-import defect):* Stale/static `phaser` import in the entry graph. **FIXED in source** by the fatal-boot-overlay + dynamic-import hotfix, and permanently gated by `tests/three/threeBootStaticGraph.test.ts`.
  2. *Hazard 2 (Environmental dep-cache drift):* Phaser is reached only through dynamic `import("phaser")` in `initGame()`. When the on-disk dep set was regenerated by a separate process, Vite pruned `phaser` from disk (the directory held only `three`, `OrbitControls`, and `@discord_embedded-app-sdk`), while the long-running dev server kept serving a stale transform of `src/main.ts` referencing `phaser.js?v=3f5a34cd` → 504. This is an environmental volatility hazard, **NOT gated by any unit/integration test**. Status: **MITIGATED — VERIFIED (2026-09-17)** via `optimizeDeps.include: ["phaser"]` in `vite.config.js:22-28`.
- **Verbatim recovery procedure (for operators/agents):**
  1. Run `pnpm exec vite optimize --force`.
  2. Confirm on-disk presence: verify that `node_modules/.vite/deps/phaser.js` exists.
  3. Re-probe the live origin via HTTP: query `/node_modules/.vite/deps/phaser.js` on `https://forest-thorne.devgovietnam.io.vn/` and assert HTTP `200`.
  4. Note: nothing is committing `dist/` — the live origin serves the working tree directly.
- **Standing HTTP probe rule for rollback route:**
  - *Historical note only:* the former URL rollback required explicit HTTP probing. Current rollback validation is branch-based, while production must prove zero Phaser boot edges.
  - *No Phase-exit gate (Phase 0, Phase 8, Phase 10) may claim "rollback route preserved" without attaching live or local HTTP probe evidence of `phaser.js` returning HTTP 200.*
- **Structural hardening & Live Verification:** `optimizeDeps.include: ["phaser"]` added in `vite.config.js:22-28`, dev-verified live (re-crawl: 289 modules / 0 failures, 4/4 deps returning 200 with fresh hashes `phaser.js?v=c4674967`, `three.js?v=908e7b72`, `OrbitControls?v=5527c1b2`, `@discord?v=18bb2bd6`; direct probe of rollback payload: 10/10 HTTP 200). Hazard 2's recurrence is structurally prevented, while detection remains governed by the standing HTTP probe rule.
## 1. Canonical source map and invariants

| Domain | Canonical source | Counterpart/host | Non-negotiable invariant |
|---|---|---|---|
| Production boot | `src/main.ts`, `src/three/ThreeApp.ts` | Standalone Three.js entrypoint booting `ThreeApp` directly | Production entry boots `ThreeApp` without URL flags, enforcing 10 gold, 3 hp, no Auto toggle, 5×10 board + 2.15 river gap, and shared runtimes |
| Scene routing | `src/three/ThreeSceneManager.ts`, `ThreeMainMenuScene`, `ThreePlanningScene`, `ThreeCombatScene` | Three scene manager routing | Full scene transitions with canonical run state preserved |
| Main menu | `src/scenes/MainMenuScene.ts` | `src/three/ThreeMainMenuScene.ts` | Direct standalone menu with Solo (AI modes), Fortress, Co-op, PvP, settings, library, and achievements |
| Planning lifecycle | `src/scenes/PlanningScene.ts`, `shared/planningSceneConstructorRuntime.ts`, `shared/planningSceneDelegateBindings.ts` | `ThreePlanningScene` host | Shared state and transition rules, separate renderer |
| Combat lifecycle | `src/scenes/CombatScene.ts`, `shared/combatSceneConstructorRuntime.ts`, `shared/combat*Runtime.ts` | `ThreeCombatScene` host | Shared queue/status/heal/damage/results rules |
| Board state | `src/systems/BoardSystem.ts`, `BoardBenchOps.ts` | Three drag host invokes same operations | Placement remains local player 5×5; no silent overwrite |
| Battlefield profile | `src/core/boardConstants.ts`, `boardProfiles.ts` | `ThreeBoard`, viewport/layout | 10 logical columns + one separate visual river gap = 11 visual columns |
| Unit visual identity | `src/ui/unitAvatarRenderer.ts`, `src/data/unitVisuals.ts`, `src/visuals/*` | Three avatar/factory renderers | Data/identity shared; renderer ownership is scene-specific |
| Status turns | `src/scenes/shared/sceneStatusTurnRuntime.ts` | Three status host adapter | `CombatScene` MUST NOT import `ThreeStatusTurnRuntime` |
| Healing | `src/scenes/shared/sceneHealUnitRuntime.ts` | Three heal host adapter | `CombatScene` MUST NOT import `ThreeHealUnitRuntime` |
| Modes/progression | `src/gameModes/*`, `src/core/gameModeRuntime.ts`, fortress/core systems | Three host counterpart | Registry and route IDs remain canonical |
| Persistence/network | `src/core/persistence.ts`, `src/scenes/shared/planningPersistenceRuntime.ts`, coop/PvP shared runtimes | Three persistence/network adapters | Envelope/schema and conflict rules are shared |

### 1.1 Board contract (must appear in every board WBS case)

- `src/core/boardConstants.ts`: `ROWS = 5`, `COLS = 10`, `PLAYER_COLS = 5`, `RIGHT_COL_START = 5`, `RIGHT_COL_END = 9`, `BOARD_GAP_COLS = 1`.
- `src/core/boardProfiles.ts`: `logicalCols = COLS = 10`; `visualCols = COLS + BOARD_GAP_COLS = 11`; `riverGapCols = 1`; enemy logical columns remain 5..9. The river is a separate visual gap, not a playable tile and not a divider collapsed onto `x = 0`.
- `SOLO_BOARD_PROFILE.rows = 5`; `COOP_BOARD_PROFILE.rows = rowsPerPlayer × 2`; `COOP4_BOARD_PROFILE.rows = rowsPerPlayer × 4`. `getPlayerRowBounds`, `getPlayerRowOffset`, `getPlayerSlotForRow`, and `getBoardVisualColCount` are authoritative.
- `src/systems/BoardSystem.ts` placement operations intentionally operate on a local 5×5 player board (`BOARD_ROWS = 5`, `BOARD_COLS = 5`, `PLAYER_COLS = 5`). A full battlefield profile and a local player placement board are different coordinate contracts; adapters MUST map them explicitly.
- No Three board case may describe a “5×10 tile board with divider at x=0”. It must describe 10 logical columns plus a separate gap and profile-aware row mapping.

### 1.2 Role color contract gap / remediation

- [~] **ROLE-COLOR-1 — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; runtime/browser evidence pending (2026-09-20).** **Gap found:** the migration contract must explicitly preserve the Phaser role palette instead of allowing Three-only hardcoded colors. Source symbols: `src/core/uiTheme.ts:CLASS_COLORS` and `ROLE_THEME`. Migration method: **M1 direct consume** these exports wherever the counterpart can import renderer-neutral constants; otherwise **M2 shared contract adapter** exposes the same typed role entries without changing values. Required fields for every role (`TANKER`, `ASSASSIN`, `ARCHER`, `MAGE`, `SUPPORT`, `FIGHTER`): `fill`, `glow`, `stroke`, `card`, `cardHover`, and `bench`. The `ASSASSIN.card` value `0x2a2146` is a mandatory sentinel. Allowed files: `src/core/uiTheme.ts` only for typed/export corrections, Three card/avatar/bench renderers, structural adapter types, and targeted parity tests. Forbidden: hardcoded Three palette replacements, role-specific fallback colors, or changing Phaser values to match existing Three visuals. Targeted test: a role-theme parity test imports the canonical source and asserts all six roles × six fields plus the `ASSASSIN.card === 0x2a2146` sentinel; renderer tests assert card, hover, bench, fill, glow, and stroke consume the mapped role entry. Runtime evidence: planning shop card, bench avatar, and combat role presentation screenshots/probe covering all roles or a deterministic fixture matrix where browser evidence is unavailable. Done: every counterpart role surface resolves through the canonical role entry, no forbidden hardcoded palette remains, and the parity test plus required runtime evidence are attached.

Implementation sequencing is UNBLOCKED to begin Phase 1.

## 1.4 SOL Feature-Parity Completion Program — authoritative completion override (2026-09-18)

> User ruling (2026-09-18): the migration target is the whole playable feature set, not only the cases named by the old plan. Existing [x] markers below are historical evidence only. They do not prove feature-complete parity. Any Phaser feature that is omitted, simplified, bypassed, test-only, or not wired into the live Three route is open again.

### 1.4.1 Completion seal — do not use new [x] markers for SOL work

- ⬜ SOL-TODO — not implemented.
- 🟨 SOL-AUDITED — canonical Phaser behavior and the current Three gap were inspected; implementation is not claimed complete.
- 🔨 SOL-CODED @ <commit> — implementation is complete and production-reachable at the cited commit. This is the user-requested code-complete seal; it is not a verification claim.
- 🧪 VERIFIED @ <commit> — <command/evidence> — optional later evidence when the environment can actually run it.
- ⛔ BLOCKED — <reason> — required authority is unavailable; never guess missing old behavior.

A 🔨 SOL-CODED seal is forbidden for a standalone file or green unit test that is not reachable from the production route beginning at src/main.ts:108-175.

### 1.4.2 Anti-omission gates — these outrank historical COMPLETE labels

1. Source-surface census: every player-facing/gameplay-affecting canonical surface under src/scenes, src/scenes/shared, src/ui, src/gameModes, src/network, src/core and src/systems must have one explicit disposition: shared SSoT consumed directly, Three presentation counterpart, rollback-only legacy surface, or justified N/A. A new unowned canonical surface blocks release.
2. Production wiring: a Three module existing in src/three does not count as migrated until a live production path reaches it. Dead adapters, callback stubs with no caller, and test-only implementations are missing features.
3. Behavior parity: model/data parity is not feature parity. Tech-node data alone does not satisfy the Tech Tree because the canonical feature also has topology, connectors, node selection, info panel, pan/zoom and research interaction at src/ui/TechTreeModal.ts:181-630.
4. Flow parity: navigation sequence is part of parity. Phaser Main Menu renders Continue/New Game first at src/scenes/MainMenuScene.ts:964-1001 and only then opens mode/difficulty selection at :1003-1208.
5. State authority: Three may not own fallback run-state, shop, unit-stat, combat, reward or routing rules when canonical shared authority exists. Current high-risk examples are ThreePlanningScene.ts:138-166,698 and ThreeCombatScene.ts:407-414,510-560,692,753.
6. Interaction parity: click, hover, right-click/context, wheel, drag, touch, keyboard/gamepad, pan/zoom, close/cancel and mobile-safe-area behavior count as part of the feature whenever Phaser exposes them.
7. No visual-only substitute: a visual shell that bypasses canonical save/session/tutorial/augment/skill/equipment/combat behavior is not migrated.
8. old_src remains immutable. The current GitHub snapshot does not contain that ignored directory, so the available canonical authority is src/scenes, src/scenes/shared, src/ui, src/core and src/systems. If a removed feature exists only in old_src, mark it BLOCKED instead of inventing behavior.
9. Coverage closure: Phase 10 cannot close while a mandatory SOL row is TODO, AUDITED or BLOCKED without an explicit user waiver.

### 1.4.3 Mandatory anti-omission census

SOL-CENSUS-01 — 🔨 SOL-CODED @ 2a5c0e170120ba214a03ed114ba723ec1d2ccdc2

Authority: live canonical feature surfaces plus the existing AST migration inventory.

Implement: extend the migration inventory tooling, or add a feature-census mode to the same generator, so every canonical surface receives a stable feature-family ID and a Three disposition. The generated census must detect:
- a canonical source added with no feature-family owner;
- a claimed Three counterpart with no production importer/wiring;
- a canonical UI/runtime family with no Three equivalent;
- duplicate Three gameplay-rule ownership.

Allowed: existing migration inventory/closure scripts, generated migration docs and tests. Forbidden: a hand-maintained list that can silently go stale.

Done for 🔨 SOL-CODED: the census is generated from source and deterministically fails on an unowned canonical surface or a dead declared counterpart.

#### SOL-P17 — Visual identity parity: unit/card/role/board semantic feedback — 🔨 SOL-CODED @ 992d90d501626e984c9146b1ec9214cfe7480bef
**Owner emoji/icon authority rewrite + shop-row cleanup — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-20.** The owner superseded the previous asset-backed icon direction: all gameplay/UI icon presentation must stop depending on curated/generated PNG/SVG icon atlases and use one shared Unicode emoji authority backed by `https://emoji-api.com/` metadata instead. Shared authority is now `src/core/emojiIcon.ts`; `src/three/ThreeEmojiIcon.ts` is a compatibility re-export. Semantic names (coin/sword/heart/settings/etc.) and raw glyphs resolve to Unicode characters; DOM controls render Unicode spans, Three.js emoji sprites/assets use generated `CanvasTexture` surfaces, combat/unit status billboards draw Unicode directly on canvas, and the current Phaser rollback `src/core/emojiAtlas.ts` renders native Unicode text instead of loading bundled Twemoji SVGs. `ThreeProductionUiAssets` keeps its old helper names only as a no-asset compatibility facade so existing call sites do not require a flag-day rewrite. The former runtime files `public/assets/ui/icons/free-icons.png`, `generated-icons.png`, and `generated-icons-extra.svg` were deleted. `VITE_EMOJI_API_KEY` is the deployment-only credential contract; the actual owner credential is intentionally not committed. emoji-api.com is metadata/Unicode authority, not an image-sprite CDN, so the runtime remains readable when the API/key is unavailable by using the locally mapped Unicode character. The redundant dark horizontal Planning shop shelf was also removed by making `.three-planning-shop-bar` and `.three-planning-shop-deck` transparent/borderless/shadowless; the owner-approved unit-card PNG remains the only card-row visual shell. Targeted source tests cover shared emoji mapping/API URL behavior, asset-free Three canvas emoji, asset-free Phaser rollback emoji, and production DOM labels. Browser/pnpm runner verification remains pending.
**Shared medieval unit-card artwork — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-20.** The owner-supplied `UIBundleFree/MediavelFree.png` sheet is cropped into production `public/assets/ui/cards/medieval-unit-card-frame.png` and `medieval-unit-card-fill.png`; the full sheet is not shipped. `ThreeUnitCardFrame.ts` applies those assets as the shared border/fill authority for live `ThreeRoundScene.updateShopCards()`, reusable `ThreePlanningShopRuntime`, and Library roster cards, so Planning and Library no longer maintain separate card backgrounds. Role colors are retained only as semantic accent/art-glow data. Regression tests pin the two crop URLs and the shared `data-unit-card-skin="medieval-bundle-free"` contract. Browser runner verification is still pending.
Authority: uiTheme.ts role palette; runeterraUnitCard.ts; unitAvatarRenderer.ts; unitVisuals.ts; board chrome/renderer and star/variant contracts. Existing ROLE-COLOR-1 remains mandatory, including the documented ASSASSIN.card sentinel.
Implement canonical semantic role/card/bench colors, identity icons, stars/traits, selection/hover/invalid feedback and ownership/decorations using true Three/DOM presentation.
Done: renderer differences are aesthetic/3D only; semantic states remain canonical.
**SUPERSEDED — historical asset-icon pass (2026-09-20).** Earlier Free Icon Pack/generated PNG/SVG atlas work is retained only as history; its runtime files were subsequently deleted by the owner-directed emoji-api/Unicode rewrite above and must not be restored as production icon authority.
Owner shop-card correction (2026-09-20): the earlier role-color-only pass was incomplete. A direct re-read of `old_src/scenes/shared/planningShopRuntime.ts`, `old_src/ui/runeterraUnitCard.ts`, and `old_src/ui/unitAvatarRenderer.ts` confirms the canonical shop card is portrait-first: role-colored art frame, a real unit visual at the portrait anchor, cost/star top badges, ATK/HP frame stats, unit name, and tribe/class identity. Live `ThreeRoundScene.updateShopCards()` and the reusable `ThreePlanningShopRuntime` now mount snapshots from the same `createUnitModel()` authority used by Library/Planning/Combat; the current factory resolves the full catalog through dedicated or generated models, so shop offers no longer degrade to text-only cards. CSS now reserves enough art height in landscape/portrait and the final medieval sweep cannot recolor the semantic card back to parchment. Regression coverage requires an avatar-preview node plus cost/star/ATK/HP/trait structure. Runtime/browser verification remains pending.
SUPERSEDED FRAME-CONSTRUCTION DETAIL (historical, before owner PNG adoption) — Dedicated card-frame follow-up (2026-09-20): per owner direction, unit/shop cards no longer participate in the shared `ThreeMedievalUiTheme` button skin. New `src/three/ThreeUnitCardFrame.ts` owns card-only framing and explicitly strips `data-medieval-button` metadata. The audited medieval `panel.png` was promoted as a dedicated binary asset at `public/assets/ui/cards/unit-card-frame.png`; ornate card corners are literal 32×32 crops from the owner-approved `MediavelFree.png` atlas, so the frame remains asset-backed rather than synthetic CSS art. Both live `ThreeRoundScene.updateShopCards()` and reusable `ThreePlanningShopRuntime` use the new util; role color remains semantic fill/accent, portrait/model rendering remains unchanged, and empty cards use the same frame with neutral semantics. CSS has a card-only override namespace keyed by `data-unit-card-frame="true"`, preventing generic medieval control rules from recoloring/reframing the card. Regression tests require no shared medieval-button metadata, the dedicated frame asset URL, four atlas-crop corner ornaments, and the portrait card structure. Runtime/browser verification remains pending.

Current gap: Three has Low/Medium/High DPR caps, adaptive downgrade and 30 FPS battery saver, but production boot does not fully restore graphics state and the user cannot directly lower render resolution independently of visual quality.
Implement:
- restore saved graphics/performance values at Three boot;
- add presentation-only render scale control that changes drawing-buffer pixel workload without changing gameplay coordinates;
- keep Low/Medium/High shadow/lighting quality independent from render scale;
- expose actual effective render scale/pixel ratio in Settings;
- preserve 30/60 FPS battery saver and adaptive quality degradation;
- make resize recompute buffer size without resetting the chosen render scale;
- never couple simulation timing to render FPS.
Animation/UX: settings changes apply immediately, with no scene restart or lost run state.
Done for 🔨 SOL-CODED: render scale visibly changes renderer pixel workload, survives reload, and can be combined with any quality tier.

- record drops once per eligible enemy death;
- carry drops through result payload;
- mutate `player.itemBag` exactly once at canonical result-commit time;
- update Endless loot achievement progress;
- show source-aware logs/result details.

  3. curved fly-to-loot/inventory target;
  4. stagger multiple drops;
  5. target pulse/count increment;
  6. animation cannot determine or duplicate gameplay state.
Done: received items are visible in Planning inventory immediately after the result transition and reload safely.

### Case 2.7b — RELEASE SPEC: slot-authorization policy and `missingSlot` rejection

**Status:** [x] **VERIFIED COMPLETE — ACCEPTED.** Caveat A is CLOSED. VERIFY verdict ACCEPT (task `01a0addb-587a-7b42-93e0-2ebbbdf13313`): 71 tests passing / 0 failed, `tsc --noEmit` exit 0, precedence-ladder checks green, SOLO P1 defaulting preserved, `coop`/`coop4` `missingSlot` hard rejection with `slot: null`, non-authorization JSDoc in place, i18n keys present in `en`/`vi`. The spec below is retained as the delivered contract of record. Case 2.8's blocker is cleared by this acceptance (it was blocked on 2.7b, not merely on the earlier explicit-slot helpers).

**Decision:** separate tile resolution from authorization. `resolveBoardTileMetadata`, `raycastBoardTile`, `resolvePointerToBoardTile`, and `resolveWorldPositionToTile` remain resolution-only and slot-agnostic when no `options.slot` is supplied. They answer “which tile is under this ray/point?”, never “may this player act here?”. The input/action boundary (`resolveInputPointerToBoardTile` plus `resolveOwnedBoardTarget*`) must require an explicit slot for multi-slot profiles and use the canonical `missingSlot` rejection. **SOLO exception:** a missing, `undefined`, `null`, or blank slot is legitimate and defaults to canonical `P1`, because `SOLO_BOARD_PROFILE.playerCount === 1`; rejecting a valid SOLO action solely for omitted slot is forbidden. Conversely, silently defaulting omitted slot to P1 on `coop`/`coop4` is forbidden.

**Exact API contract (DEV MUST implement exactly):**

| API | Required 2.7b delta |
|---|---|
| `BoardRaycastOptions` | Keep `slot?: string` optional for backward-compatible resolution calls. Clarify that it is an optional ownership filter, not required resolution context; `null`/blank is treated as omitted. Do not make it required globally. |
| `resolveBoardTileMetadata` | Signature and return remain `(target: unknown, profile?: BoardProfileKind \| BoardProfile, options?: BoardRaycastOptions) \| BoardRaycastTarget \| null`. Keep omitted-slot behavior slot-agnostic, including the existing COOP2 repro; when a slot is supplied, retain ownership filtering. Add the mandatory non-authorization JSDoc below. |
| `raycastBoardTile` | Signature and return remain unchanged. Forward `options` unchanged to metadata resolution; do not synthesize P1 or emit `missingSlot` here. It is resolution-only. |
| `resolvePointerToBoardTile` | Signature and return remain unchanged. Forward `options` unchanged; omitted slot remains allowed because this is the generic resolution helper, not the action gate. |
| `resolveWorldPositionToTile` | Signature and return remain unchanged. Forward `options` unchanged; no authorization decision or `missingSlot` is emitted here. |
| `resolveInputPointerToBoardTile` | Preserve the existing `BoardRaycastTarget \| null` return and existing valid-pointer/invalid-pointer callers. Before raycast/action acceptance, resolve profile player count: `coop`/`coop4` with absent (`undefined`, `null`, or blank) `options.slot` returns `null` (hard rejection) and MUST be observable as `missingSlot` through the new result companion `resolveInputPointerToBoardTileResult` (same arguments, return `BoardOwnershipResolution`). SOLO absent slot defaults to P1 and retains current target/null behavior. No compatibility-breaking replacement of the existing return union. |
| `resolveOwnedBoardTarget` | Change the slot parameter to accept an optional nullable slot (`slot?: string \| null`) while preserving `OwnedBoardTarget \| null`. For SOLO, absent/blank slot resolves as P1; for `coop`/`coop4`, absent/blank slot returns `null` before metadata resolution. An explicit slot still proves ownership with `isRowOwnedByPlayer`. |
| `resolveOwnedBoardTargetResult` | Change slot to `slot?: string \| null`, preserve `BoardOwnershipResolution`, and return exactly one `missingSlot` signal for absent/blank slot on `coop`/`coop4`; SOLO defaults to P1. Do not collapse this result to `null`. |
| `classifyBoardTargetRejection` | Change slot to `slot?: string \| null`. For multi-slot profiles, absent/blank slot returns `missingSlot`; for SOLO it normalizes to P1. Preserve `null` for valid owned grid cells and existing `foreignRow`/`outOfBounds` results. Apply the precedence order below. |
| `isBoardTargetOwnedBySlot` | Change slot to `slot?: string \| null`. Return `false` for absent/blank slot on `coop`/`coop4`; treat it as P1 only for SOLO. This remains a boolean ownership predicate and MUST NOT manufacture a rejection signal. |
| `BoardRejectionSignal` | Change `slot` from `string` to `string \| null`. A `missingSlot` signal MUST carry `slot: null`; it MUST NOT normalize or serialize the missing value as fallback `P1`. |
| `createBoardRejectionSignal` | Change the signature to `(profile: BoardProfileKind \| BoardProfile, reason: BoardRejectionReason, slot?: string \| null)`. Normalize a non-blank supplied slot; serialize absent/blank slot as `null`, including for `missingSlot`. Existing explicit-slot signals retain their normalized slot. |
| `resolveInputPointerToBoardTileResult` (new companion) | Add the result-form action resolver with the same pointer/camera/mesh/profile/bounds/options inputs as `resolveInputPointerToBoardTile`, returning `BoardOwnershipResolution`. It MUST return `missingSlot` with `rejection.slot === null` for omitted/blank slot on `coop`/`coop4`, preserve SOLO P1 defaulting, and otherwise map raycast/ownership failures to exactly one canonical rejection signal. The compatibility resolver delegates to it and returns target-or-null. |

**Mandatory resolution JSDoc (verbatim on `resolveBoardTileMetadata`; repeat on the three generic wrappers):** “Resolution only, NOT authorization: this function identifies the tile under a ray/point and MUST NOT be used as permission to mutate board state. Action paths MUST use an explicit player slot and an ownership-gated resolver.” Also state: “When `options.slot` is omitted, resolution remains slot-agnostic by design; this is not proof of ownership.”

**Deterministic rejection precedence (highest to lowest; tests MUST assert collisions):** (1) `missingSlot` when the action profile has more than one player and slot is absent/blank; (2) `river` when a supplied world point or closest hit is the river divider/corridor; (3) `outOfBounds` for null/non-object targets, missing/non-numeric/non-finite/non-integer row or col, or row/col outside the resolved profile; (4) `foreignRow` when coordinates are valid but `isRowOwnedByPlayer(profile,row,slot)` is false; (5) accept. Thus a multi-slot river point with no slot returns `missingSlot`; with an explicit slot it returns `river`; a multi-slot `{row: null, col: 0}` with no slot returns `missingSlot`, while with `P1` it returns `outOfBounds`. No fallback `P1` authorization is permitted on multi-slot profiles.

**i18n:** add `three.board.rejected.missingSlot` to both `src/i18n/en.ts` and `src/i18n/vi.ts`, beside the existing `foreignRow|outOfBounds|river` keys at their current `:619-623` ranges. Required key identity is exact: `BOARD_REJECTION_I18N_KEYS.missingSlot === "three.board.rejected.missingSlot"`; both locale maps MUST define it. Suggested copy: EN “Select a player slot before placing.” / VI “Hãy chọn ô người chơi trước khi đặt.”

**Backward compatibility:** the three pre-existing pointer resolver tests in `tests/three/threeBoardInputController.test.js` remain unchanged and passing: valid SOLO pointer resolution still yields a target or `null`, null/non-object pointers still yield `null`, and the four generic resolver exports remain functions. The existing 48-test VERIFY scope and its 19-test companion scope retain their current behavior; the 48-test scope is not rewritten to require a slot. Generic resolution calls without a slot remain valid for SOLO and remain slot-agnostic for direct metadata/raycast helpers. Only action-path calls on `coop`/`coop4` gain the hard missing-slot rejection.

**2.7b test matrix (DEV MUST add):**
1. `resolveBoardTileMetadata({row:7,col:2}, "coop")` remains a non-authorizing resolution and may return `playerSlot="P2", rowOffset=5`; the same target through `resolveOwnedBoardTargetResult("coop", ..., undefined)`, `null`, and `""` returns `{accepted:false,target:null,rejection.reason:"missingSlot"}`.
2. Omitted slot on `coop4` through both action resolvers returns `missingSlot`; explicit `P1`/`P4` valid owned rows still accept, and foreign rows still return `foreignRow`.
3. Omitted, `undefined`, `null`, and blank slot on SOLO valid `{row:0,col:0}` all default to P1 and accept; the same forms remain backward-compatible for generic pointer resolution. A SOLO missing-slot rejection is forbidden.
4. Precedence: no-slot coop/coop4 river world point → `missingSlot`; explicit-slot river point → `river`; no-slot invalid/out-of-range row → `missingSlot`; explicit-slot `null`, `undefined`, `-Infinity`, `-0.5`, and out-of-profile rows → `outOfBounds`; valid but foreign row with explicit slot → `foreignRow`.
5. Assert `BOARD_REJECTION_I18N_KEYS.missingSlot` and both locale translations; assert no mutation/signal multiplicity: one result, one reason, no board/bench/UID mutation.
6. Assert the three existing pointer resolver tests and the 48-test scope unchanged, plus the new result companion's target/null parity with the compatibility wrapper.

**Why this unblocks 2.8:** 2.7b gives every invalid action a deterministic, localized reason before placement mutation: omitted multi-player authorization becomes `missingSlot`, while river, bounds, and foreign-row cases retain their canonical precedence. Case 2.8 can therefore wire its required exactly-one localized rejection event and no-mutation/ghost-object assertions without guessing whether a generic raycast result was also an authorization decision.

### Case 2.12 — COOP4 camera framing clamped by OrbitControls `maxDistance` (FIXED & VERIFIED 2026-09-17; opened as a measured defect by PLAN the same day)

- [x] **2.12 — COOP4 framing clamp — FIXED & VERIFIED 2026-09-17.** **Source symbol:** `src/three/ThreeCameraManager.ts:31` (`BASE_MAX_DISTANCE = 50`) + `:36` (`DOLLY_HEADROOM = 5`) feeding `:213-221` (`setDollyCeiling(d) = Math.max(BASE_MAX_DISTANCE, d + DOLLY_HEADROOM)`; `:220` is the sole writer of `controls.maxDistance` in `src/`), called from the constructor (`:79-81`) and from `frameBoardProfile` (`:189-193`, computing `targetDist = Math.max(baseDistance, config.pos[1])`; the removed pre-fix clamp was `:70-71`), reached from `src/three/ThreeApp.ts:68-75` (`setBoardProfile`). **Migration:** M4 presentation. **Allowed:** `ThreeCameraManager.ts` and its tests. **Forbidden:** hiding the defect by shrinking the computed distance, or keeping the mock-echo camera assertion that asserts the mock's own formula. **Targeted Vitest:** `threePlanningProfile.test.ts` — the mock-echo assertion was replaced — `:166-200` drives the REAL `ThreeCameraManager` (only the GL renderer faked) and asserts the ceiling plus worst |NDC| < 1 across all three profiles — plus a camera-manager suite. **Runtime evidence:** projected NDC containment of the extreme rows per profile with the real camera manager. **Measured (2026-09-17, PLAN probe, real `ThreeApp` + real `ThreeCameraManager`, GL renderer faked, 60 frames @ 1/60 s after each switch):** solo → cameraDist 18.0000, worst `|ndcY|` 0.6605 (fits); coop → 35.0363, 0.6893 (fits); **coop4 → cameraDist clamped to 50.0000 while `frameBoardProfile` computes ≈70.07, worst `|ndcY|` = 1.0809 → the outer rows of the 20-row board overflow the frustum by ~8%.** **Root cause:** the transition target is ≈70.07 but `OrbitControls.update()` clamps the radius to `maxDistance = 50` on every post-transition frame, so the computed framing is unreachable. **Done:** COOP4 (20 rows) frames inside NDC [-1, 1] with the real camera manager, and the committed test proves it with the real camera path instead of a mock. **Blocks:** nothing in Phase 3 — this is a COOP4 presentation defect.

**Dependency:** Phase 2. **Authority:** `planningDragRuntime.ts`, `planningBoardInteractionRuntime.ts`, `planningInputRuntime.ts`, `planningFormationRuntime.ts`, `BoardSystem.ts`, `BoardBenchOps.ts`. **Counterparts:** `ThreePlanningDragRuntime.ts`, `ThreeBoardInputController.ts`, `ThreePlanningScene.ts`, `ThreeUnitAvatarRenderer.ts`.

**PLAN Phase 3 audit (2026-09-17, task `01a0aee2-879d-78e1-b220-f1190f4ac9b0`).** Every case below was crosswalked against the live Phaser sources (`src/systems/BoardBenchOps.ts`, `src/systems/BoardSystem.ts`, `src/scenes/shared/planning{Formation,Drag,Input,BoardInteraction}Runtime.ts`, `src/scenes/shared/planningCoopSessionRuntime.ts`, `src/core/boardProfiles.ts`, `src/core/uiResponsive.ts`) and against every existing Three suite. Baseline command: `pnpm exec vitest --run --maxWorkers=2 tests/three/threePlanningDragIntegration.test.ts tests/three/threePlanningDragRuntime.test.js tests/three/threePlanningInputRuntime.test.ts tests/three/threeInputUnification.test.ts tests/three/threeBenchLayout.test.ts tests/three/threeMobileTouchTarget.test.js` → **7 files / 45 tests passed, 0 failed** (historical pre-dedup baseline: this command listed the `.ts`/`.js` twins of the same suite, so the post-dedup command covers 6 distinct files and the count was not re-measured). Result: **3.3** and **3.6** are already satisfied → `[x]`; the other 13 cases stay `[ ]` with the exact missing assertion named in their **GAP** field. DEV must not re-implement covered behaviour — implement only the GAP. A case line without a Phaser `file:line` source is not released for DEV. **Addendum (2026-09-17, task `01a0b031-9375-7320-8ec2-c63801b0fb67`, framing settled):** case **3.8** is a **phantom gap** on the canonical recall path — with a **compact** bench no input yields `"Bench is full"`, and `:54` is correct cap enforcement written for that representation. Sub-case **3.8b** is a **LIVE DEFECT**, now proved by measurement rather than argument: `tests/three/threePlanningShopIntegration.test.ts` went **3 failed / 5 passed before the fix** (starter still on `board[2][2]` after the recall attempt) to **8 passed (8) after**, on a scene built through the live entrypoint with no injected `bench`. The invariant that first appeared to refute the defect is retained and completed: adding **`ensureStartingUnits`** to its writer list makes `L + U = 10 > cap` at run start, which predicts the defect. Sub-case **3.8c** was split out for the bench-cap authority divergence (Three 9 vs Phaser planning 8/14), which is gameplay-visible and must stay separately attributable. Withdrawn and not to be restored: “defensive / unreachable”, “latent hazard, not a live defect”, “reachable only by direct construction”, “do not describe this as an open production bug”. DEV's fix is in the **working tree only** — HEAD still has the defect — so PLAN re-pins `src/three/ThreePlanningScene.ts` numbers only after DEV's real freeze.
