- [x] **3.8 — Board→bench valid/full.** **Source symbol:** `src/systems/BoardBenchOps.ts:39` (`moveBoardUnitToBench`) and `src/scenes/shared/planningFormationRuntime.ts:643` (`getPlanningBenchCap`) / `src/three/ThreeBoard.ts:408` (`setBenchCap`). **Migrate:** M2 — `src/three/ThreePlanningScene.ts:1016-1018` (`:1017`) calls `moveBoardUnitToBench(this.board, this.bench, from.row, from.col, to.index, BENCH_DEFAULT_CAP + (this.player.benchBonus ?? 0))`. **Allowed:** bench adapter and tests. **Forbidden:** clearing the board before capacity validation. **Targeted Vitest:** `tests/three/threeBenchShop.test.js:88-93` (valid recall: `success`, board cell `null`, bench receives the unit) and `:102-121` (`:106-109` → `"Invalid bench index"` via the `BoardBenchOps.ts:49` index guard; `:112-115` → `"Bench position occupied and swap not allowed"` via `:53`; both leave `board[0][0]` unchanged), plus the drag path at `tests/three/threePlanningDragIntegration.test.ts:280-298` (Case 3.8 block: `:281-288` rejects bench index 99 through the drag callback, `:290-298` performs the valid recall with the two state assertions at `:297` `board[1][1]` is null and `:298` `bench[1] === "fox_flame"`). **Note:** `src/three/ThreePlanningScene.ts:1017` returns only `.success`, so the drag suite cannot detect a move-vs-swap regression — `threeBenchShop.test.js:91` (`expect(recallRes.swapped).toBe(false)`) is the only pin on `swapped`. **Required added assertion:** none that is reachable — the capacity branch is a phantom gap (see below); the only genuine residue is that `src/three/ThreePlanningScene.ts:1017` returns only `.success`, so no drag-path test can distinguish a move from a swap (pinned instead by `threeBenchShop.test.js:91`). **Runtime evidence:** compact-bench recall probe. **Done:** on a **canonically compact** bench: (i) a valid recall clears the board cell and places the unit in the bench slot; (ii) a recall into an occupied slot swaps under the default `allowSwap = true` and reports `swapped: true`, and is refused under `allowSwap = false` with `"Bench position occupied and swap not allowed"` while conserving both sides. A **contiguously full occupied** bench is NOT a capacity-rejection case: LEAD measured 9 real units + recall to idx 2 → `success`/`swapped`, matching `threeBenchShop.test.js:117-122`. **GAP (2026-09-17, PHANTOM GAP — supersedes two earlier framings of this row):** this case as originally framed had **no reachable input**, so it is a *phantom gap*, not an under-tested branch. The correct statement has two halves and neither may be dropped. **(1) Under the canonical compact representation `src/systems/BoardBenchOps.ts:54` is unreachable.** The guard `if (!target && bench.length >= benchCap)` keys off **array length**, and compaction plus `length >= cap` implies every index `< cap` is occupied, so `target` is non-null and `!target` is false; `:49` (`isValidBenchIndex`, `src/systems/BoardSystem.ts:195-197`) already forces the index below `cap`. `:54` is therefore **correct cap enforcement written FOR the compact representation, and a no-op there** — it enforces the invariant rather than covering a reachable behaviour, so **no canonical input yields `"Bench is full"`**. Phaser keeps compaction absolutely: `src/core/runState.ts:366` (`bench: []`), `:422-424` (hydrate drops non-present entries), `src/scenes/shared/planningFormationRuntime.ts:449` and `:469` (`bench.splice(index, 1)`), `src/scenes/CombatScene.ts:3204` (`splice`), and the op's own writers `src/systems/BoardBenchOps.ts:35` (deploy — `splice`), `:57`/`:60-62` (recall — non-null assignment, `push`, or splice-insert). **(2) The only state that reaches it must be built by hand** — a bench holding a `null` at a valid index while `length >= cap` yields `"Bench is full"` — and **no play sequence produces that state**, including the pre-sized all-null bench at `src/three/ThreePlanningScene.ts:202-214` (an all-null bench has no unit to recall, so `:51` returns first). The state that reaches it is not merely unreachable — it was **produced in production** by the pre-sized bench, which is the live defect tracked as Case **3.8b**; the hole-at-capacity fixture therefore characterises a real, reproduced failure, not a hypothetical one. **Superseded wording (withdrawn, do not restore):** “defensive — unreachable from the production paths” (imprecise: it is unreachable *canonically*, and unreachable *by invariant* — see 3.8b); “a recall into a bench with no free slot returns failure” (wrong: a contiguously full bench swaps successfully); and the three claims introduced by the withdrawn revisions — “this is a **latent hazard**, not a live defect”, “the reaching state is only reachable **by direct construction** and no play sequence produces it”, and “**do not describe this as an open production bug**” — **all three are withdrawn**: the round-1 starter grant makes `:54` fire on the player's first recall (see 3.8b). **Restored as CORRECT:** “it fires at run start” — that is precisely what the pre-fix failure shows (`board[2][2]` still occupied after the recall attempt). **Evidence provenance:** the reachability measurements are **LEAD-run** (direct `moveBoardUnitToBench` import, no build, no browser); PLAN verified every cited `file:line` above by direct read on 2026-09-17 — no outstanding DEV check marker. **VERIFIED 2026-09-17 (Phantom gap — verified in 01a0aff8 / 01a0b034):** valid recall and index limits verified; capacity rejection is a phantom gap canonically unreachable on compact bench.
- [x] **3.8b — Round-1 recall reports `"Bench is full"` on an EMPTY bench (LIVE DEFECT — measured; fix landed in working tree, freeze-pinned).** **Severity: LIVE, user-visible on the first interaction of a new run.** Root cause: the Three planning scene pre-sized its bench to a cap-length array of nulls while the guard at `src/systems/BoardBenchOps.ts:54` keys off **array `length`, not occupancy**. **Measured production state (LEAD, on pre-fix tree):** `bench.length` at round-1 start **9**, cap **9**, **0 of 9 slots occupied**, and yet `moveBoardUnitToBench` for the round-1 starter returned `{"success":false,"error":"Bench is full"}` for destination `bench[0]`, `bench[2]` and `bench[4]`. **Evidence chain (every link verified on frozen revision `ThreePlanningScene.ts` md5 `d8936eaf866330c376b470c188619e62`, 1095 lines):** `src/main.ts:165` starts the run with `bench = undefined`; the scene builds the bench at `ThreePlanningScene.ts:202-214` (HEAD numbering `:172`) with `activeBenchCap = BENCH_DEFAULT_CAP + player.benchBonus` (`src/three/types.ts:123` → 9) and the board empty at `:201`; the constructor then calls `ensureStartingUnits()` at `:233` (def `:617-646`), whose body scans the board and the bench (`:619-629`), and on `!hasUnits` (`:631`) grants a starter **directly onto the board** at `:644` (`this.board[2][2] = starter`) — **not through a deploy, so the bench array is untouched and still length 9**. Both recall entry points then feed that array and the matching cap into the guard: the bench-click handler `:420-430` (`:422`) and the drag commit `:1016-1018` (`:1017`, gated by the `.success` return). `:54` therefore fires on a **board→empty-bench-slot recall at run start**: the starter cannot be recalled, with zero units on the bench. **Window — a soft deadlock:** from run start until the first successful bench→board deploy. `placeBenchUnitOnBoard:35` (`bench.splice(benchIndex, 1)`) would drop `length` below cap and restore recall, but the bench is EMPTY, so there is nothing to deploy; the player must first **buy** a unit, and a purchase writes into a null slot **without changing `length`**, so the guard still fires afterwards — the deadlock only breaks once that purchased unit is itself deployed. **State dependence (why the failure is confined to run start):** two independent mechanisms. **(a)** The first successful bench→board deploy compacts the array (`BoardBenchOps.ts:35` `bench.splice(benchIndex, 1)`), after which `L < cap` and recall works again. **(b)** Only the FIRST planning scene of a run is built without a bench: `src/main.ts:165` passes `undefined` for both `board` and `bench`, whereas every later transition passes real state — `:131` (`startPlanning(updatedPlayer, combatData.board, combatData.bench, nextRound)`) and `:134` (`onRetry`). From round 2 onward the scene receives combat's bench, so the guard is unreachable there. **Representation stickiness:** the same array is threaded **by reference** for the whole run (see Round boundary below), so a pre-sized array would persist across rounds — which is why the fix normalises **in place** rather than copying. **The invariant, completed — it PROVES this defect (it must not be read as a refutation):** let `L = bench.length` and `U` = units on the board. Every board↔bench writer preserves or lowers `L + U`: `placeBenchUnitOnBoard` onto an EMPTY cell (`BoardBenchOps.ts:35` `bench.splice`) does `L-1, U+1`; onto an OCCUPIED cell (`:34`) leaves both; a recall via `push` (`:60`) does `L+1, U-1`; via hole assignment (`:61`) `L, U-1`; `moveBenchUnit` (`:80-85`) leaves `L`; `removeOwnedUnitRefs` (`:113`) and the merge repack (`src/systems/UpgradeMergeRuntime.ts:53`, `:57-58`) only lower it; a shop purchase appends into the compact bench (`ThreePlanningShopActionsRuntime.ts:139`). **The writer list MUST also include `ensureStartingUnits`, which the first version of this row omitted:** the constructor calls it (`ThreePlanningScene.ts:233`), and on `!hasUnits` it assigns `this.board[2][2] = starter` (`:644`) — `U: 0 → 1` while `L` stays at the pre-sized 9. At run start `L + U = 10 > cap = 9`; `:54` needs `L >= cap` **and** `U >= 1` (otherwise `:51` returns first), i.e. `L + U >= cap + 1`, which is **satisfied**. The invariant therefore *predicts* the defect instead of refuting it — and it explains why a model built from the two board↔bench ops alone (3 runs, ~116,000 recalls) saw zero fires: that model never performed the grant. **Superseded reasoning (withdrawn):** an earlier revision of this row declared this invariant “wrong” and “withdrawn”. The invariant was right; only its writer list was incomplete. Any model of this subsystem must include the grant path. **Severity vocabulary:** an intermediate revision called this a *latent hazard* and forbade describing it as a bug — **that revision is withdrawn**; it is a live defect, and the pre-fix behaviour is the evidence. **Runtime evidence (LEAD-measured on the real scene through the live entrypoint, not unit-mocked):** a scene built the way `src/main.ts:165` builds it — **no injected `bench`** — via `tests/three/threePlanningShopIntegration.test.ts`: **3 failed / 5 passed BEFORE the fix**, the first failure being `expected { uid: 'recall-hero', ... } to be null`, i.e. after `onTileClick(2,2)` + `onBenchClick(0)` the starter was **still on `board[2][2]`**, proving the recall was rejected; and **8 passed (8) AFTER the fix**. That red→green pair is both the proof of the defect and its regression guard. **Fix status (landed in working tree on frozen revision):** the coordinated three-site fix is present: the scene init at `ThreePlanningScene.ts:202-214` keeps a compact bench (`this.bench = data.bench ?? []`, plus an **in-place** `splice(0, length, ...filter(...))` compaction at `:212-214` so callers already holding the array stay in sync — the aliasing hazard below) with a comment naming the round-1 starter as the reproduction; `ThreePlanningShopActionsRuntime.ts:104-112` (md5 `97730c267557df78eb730a023426f9b4`, 222 lines) replaced the `findIndex((slot) => slot === null)` free-slot lookup with the length-vs-cap check at `:109` (Phaser parity `planningShopActionsRuntime.ts:137-138`), appends purchases by `push` at `:139`, and re-packs on auto-merge by `splice` + `push` at `:156-159`; the bench UI at `ThreePlanningScene.ts:907` now reads `computePlanningBenchLayout(width, height, BENCH_DEFAULT_CAP + (this.player.benchBonus ?? 0))` instead of `this.bench.length || 8`. **All three were required together** — compacting the bench alone would have made every purchase fail. **Round boundary (closed, still true):** `src/three/ThreeCombatScene.ts` never reads or writes the board — grep for `player.board`, `board[` and `this.board` returns **zero hits** — and `ThreePlanningScene.ts:565-570` hands `player`/`board`/`bench`/`round` to `onStartCombat` **by reference**, with `src/main.ts:131` (`onNextRound`) and `:134` (`onRetry`) re-entering with those same arrays. Round 2+ is therefore a pure carry-over — and because the arrays are **aliased**, any representation change must be evaluated across both scenes (this aliasing is why the in-place compaction at `ThreePlanningScene.ts:212-214` matters). **Silent rejection (separate defect, cross-ref task `01a0b01d`):** nothing on the Three side surfaced the reason. The drag path returns only `.success` (`:1016-1018` → `:1017`) inside a `dragCallbacks` body (`:1004-1026`) that supplies no `addLog`; the bench-click path calls the op and **discards the result** (`:420-430`, no branch on outcome); the bench→board selection path tests only `res.success` (`:372-381`). Phaser, by contrast, emits an explicit hint (`src/scenes/shared/planningFormationRuntime.ts:338-352`, guard `:305-308`). A live defect therefore produced **no user-visible reason** — which is why it survived until now. **Why every suite missed it:** each suite injects the bench (or a non-empty board) at construction — `tests/three/threePlanningDragIntegration.test.ts:90-96`, `tests/three/threePlanningShopIntegration.test.ts:122-124` — so a run-start scene with **no** `bench` and **no** board units, which is the only configuration that reaches the grant at `ThreePlanningScene.ts:644`, was never exercised. **Migrate:** M2 — keep the landed shape: one fullness model (compact bench, `length` vs cap) shared by the scene init, the shop and the UI, with `ensureStartingUnits` left untouched (it is correct once the bench is compact). **Allowed:** the Three planning scene's bench initialization, the Three shop action runtime, the bench UI layout call, and their tests. **Forbidden:** re-introducing a pre-sized/null-padded bench; a Three-side capacity rule that disagrees with `BoardBenchOps`; restoring the `null`-identity free-slot lookup; or fixing only one of the three sites. **Targeted Vitest:** `tests/three/threeBenchShop.test.js:216-263` — the hole-at-capacity fixture (`:230-249`, compact contrast `:250-263`), which characterises a state that is not reachable under the compact rules — plus a run-start construction test: `new ThreePlanningScene({ player })` with **no** `bench` and an empty board, asserting (i) the starter exists at `board[2][2]`, (ii) recalling it into an empty bench slot succeeds, (iii) a purchase succeeds and (iv) the purchased unit is addressable at the index the shop reported. **Done:** a scene constructed for round 1 with no `bench` performs buy → deploy → recall without any consumer reporting “full” while a slot is free, and the bench array stays compact (`length === occupancy`) throughout. **Cap scope (LEAD decision, 2026-09-17): this fix must NOT touch the cap stack.** Its only cap requirement is that the three consumers read the **same cap expression** — the scene init, the shop free-slot check and the bench UI all use `BENCH_DEFAULT_CAP + (this.player.benchBonus ?? 0)`, which they now do, and **no cap value may change here**. The separate divergence between that expression and Phaser's planning authority `getPlanningBenchCap` is **tracked as its own case, 3.8c**, because it changes visible player behaviour (bench slot count) and must remain separately attributable. **VERIFIED 2026-09-17 (PLAN-VERIFY ACCEPT in task 01a0b034-3201-7a52-8739-90ff5362fcd5):** Live defect fix landed, in-place compaction verified, full sweep 112/112 files 682/682 tests pass 100%.
- [~] **3.8c — Planning bench cap authority divergence (Three 9 vs Phaser 8/14) — gameplay-visible, separate case.** **This is deliberately NOT part of 3.8b.** LEAD decision 2026-09-17: the representation fix must not touch the cap stack, so that a later gameplay change in bench slot count stays separately attributable. **Source symbol / divergence:** the Three planning path computes its cap as `BENCH_DEFAULT_CAP + (player.benchBonus ?? 0)` (`src/three/types.ts:123` → 9, read at the scene init `ThreePlanningScene.ts:202-214`, at the shop free-slot check `ThreePlanningShopActionsRuntime.ts:108-112` and at the bench UI layout `ThreePlanningScene.ts:907`) and this matches `src/scenes/CombatScene.ts:3244-3246` (`getBenchCap() = 9 + this.player.benchBonus`). The Phaser **planning** authority is a different function: `src/scenes/shared/planningFormationRuntime.ts:643-650` (`getPlanningBenchCap`) returns **`(level > 0 ? 14 : 8) + bonus`**, and subtracts 1 in creative mode (`Math.max(1, baseCap - 1)`), where `level = scene.player.benchUpgradeLevel`. Phaser also applies that cap on the planning path — the shop's fullness check at `src/scenes/shared/planningShopActionsRuntime.ts:137` (`bench.length >= getPlanningBenchCap(scene)`) and the recall call at `planningFormationRuntime.ts:182-188` / `:325-333` — so the two runtimes do not agree on how many bench slots a planning screen has. **Why it matters:** this changes **visible player behaviour** (how many slots the bench renders and how many units a planning screen accepts), not just an internal guard. It is a parity question against rule 3 (Phaser is source of truth) and must be resolved by measurement, not by preference. **Migrate:** M2 — whichever cap wins, the Three planning scene, the Three shop and the bench UI must all consume one expression derived from the same authority (`getPlanningBenchCap` semantics, including the upgrade-level branch and the creative-mode decrement), rather than the current constant. **Allowed:** the Three cap constant and its three read sites, plus tests. **Forbidden:** changing `src/systems/**`; changing the cap inside the 3.8b representation fix; or picking a value without recording the measurement that justifies it. **Targeted Vitest:** a parity test that builds the same player state (`benchUpgradeLevel` 0 and > 0, creative on and off, `benchBonus` 0 and > 0) and asserts that the Three planning cap equals `getPlanningBenchCap` of the Phaser authority for every combination; plus a slot-render assertion that the bench UI shows exactly that many slots. **Done:** for every measured combination of `benchUpgradeLevel`, `benchBonus` and creative mode, the Three planning scene, its shop and its bench UI report the **same** cap as `getPlanningBenchCap`, and the rendered slot count follows it. **Status:** RESOLVED / CODED — Planning now consumes the renderer-neutral `resolvePlanningBenchCap` contract extracted from the canonical shared Planning semantics: 8 before upgrade, 14 after upgrade, additive `benchBonus`, and Creative reserves one slot. Three Planning uses that contract for its live cap and renders the full upgraded 16-slot visual pool when 14+2 is active. Regression matrix added at `tests/core/planningBenchCap.test.ts` in commit `8a55e430e8ec638f77d982bab7a848cc8b5d21d3`. Runtime execution remains part of P20 and is not implied by this source-level closure.

  **CANONICAL CONTRACT — grid SIZE authority (DIVERGENCE 1, recorded 2026-09-17; read-verified by PLAN against `src/scenes/shared/planningCraftRuntime.ts` md5 `75c3260e42dd3ac15b0e53f94ff8b63d`):** `getPlanningCraftGridSize` reads **only** `scene.player.craftTableLevel` — `>=3 → 3`, `>=2 → 2`, `>=1 → 1`, **else `0`** (`:34-40`), so the level→size mapping is `0→0, 1→1, 2→2, 3→3`. **`techLevels.craft_t` is NOT an input of grid size.** The tech `craft_t` (`src/data/techTree.ts:290-300`: `effects: [{ type: "craft_table_upgrade", value: 1 }]`, `maxBuy: 3`) acts on the grid **only** by raising `craftTableLevel` upstream — the tech effect `craft_table_upgrade` at `src/scenes/shared/planningTechRuntime.ts:176-179` (`scene.player.craftTableLevel = Math.min(3, (scene.player.craftTableLevel || 0) + 1)`), the paid upgrade path at `:262-272` (cost table `[5, 10, 15]` at `:262-263`; after payment `scene.player.craftTableLevel = level + 1` at `:269`; log label table `["1x1","2x2","3x3"]` at `:270-271` indexed by the **pre-purchase** level, so paying at level 0 yields the 1x1 table — consistent with `0→0`), and the round-6 tutorial grant `src/scenes/shared/planningTutorialRuntime.ts:209` (`Math.max(1, scene.player.craftTableLevel ?? 0)`). Field authority: `src/core/runState.ts:121` (type), `:235` (default **0**), `:494-496` (hydrate: `Math.max(0, Math.min(3, Math.floor(source.craftTableLevel)))`, missing/non-finite → `0`) and `src/scenes/shared/planningRunStateRuntime.ts:466-467` (sanitize + `clamp(…, 0, 3)`). **`size === 0` is a real authored state, not an error case:** the canonical commit gate refuses it with a dedicated localized message — `planningCraftRuntime.ts:196-199` (`const tableSize = getPlanningCraftGridSize(scene); if (tableSize <= 0) { addPlanningLog(…, t("planning.craft.tableNotUnlocked")); return false; }`), key present in both locales (`src/i18n/vi.ts:671`, `src/i18n/en.ts:671`). **Therefore Three MUST NOT default to 1×1 at level 0, and MUST NOT consult `techLevels.craft_t` for the grid dimension.**

**Owner combat-readability follow-up — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-20.** Static audit found the ambiguous red circle in `ThreeCombatVfxRuntime.spawnPulse()`: impact/low-HP feedback was rendered with `THREE.RingGeometry` directly around the unit base, so it could be misread as a persistent attacker/target/selection marker. The production combat pulse now copies the established ground-language from `ThreeRoundScene.ensureTurnScanIndicator()` (the purple scan indicator): a translucent ground-plane tile plus `EdgesGeometry` border, using the existing damage-type color (physical remains red) and a short scale/fade animation. No combat targeting, damage, queue or state rule changed. `tests/three/threeCombatVfx.test.ts` pins a `PlaneGeometry + LineSegments` ground highlight and rejects `RingGeometry` for this pulse. That first pass was later superseded by the owner follow-up below: circular unit indicators are now removed across the live Three unit/VFX stack. Runner/browser verification is still required before upgrading this follow-up from source evidence to runtime evidence.

- [~] **8.1 — PRE-CLEANUP EVIDENCE INVALIDATED; RE-RUN REQUIRED ON 2026-09-22+ HEAD. — Direct AST inventory.** **Source symbol:** generator AST extraction and 100-row partition. **Migration:** M1. **Allowed:** generator/docs. **Forbidden:** omitted files or stale count; conflating static `from "phaser"` with dynamic `import("phaser")`, which previously emitted a false static-import row for `src/main.ts` (actual form: `import("phaser")` at `src/main.ts:197`). **Targeted Vitest:** `threeProductionReleaseGate.test.ts` plus generator command. **Runtime evidence:** generated artifact diff. **Done:** live files, 98 direct runtime rows, 2 structural rows, and 100 total reconcile (measured: `pnpm run inventory:three-migration` → `98 runtime + 2 structural adapters = 100 total tracked files`). **Audited 2026-09-18:** generator output reconciles 100/100; `threeProductionBuildVerification.test.ts` confirms zero AST Phaser imports in `src/three/**`.
- [~] **8.2 — PRE-CLEANUP EVIDENCE INVALIDATED; RE-RUN REQUIRED ON 2026-09-22+ HEAD. — Transitive import closure.** **Source symbol:** generator `imports`, `importTargetCandidates`, and new closure traversal. **Migration:** M3. **Allowed:** generator and import-graph tests. **Forbidden:** claiming "Pure Three dependencies: 0" from direct-token count; allowing `src/three/** → src/scenes/** → phaser` reverse edges; silently skipping unresolved specifiers. **Known audit prerequisites:** Three adapters re-export shared scene cores (`ThreeBoardInputController.ts`, `ThreeBoardViewport.ts`); `ThreeStatusTurnRuntime.ts` and `ThreeHealUnitRuntime.ts` import shared Phaser runtimes; `ThreeLoadingScene.ts` imports `loadingBubbleMinigameRuntime.ts`; `src/scenes/shared/planningCombatTransitionRuntime.ts` imports `sceneLazyRouting.ts`; generator reports 18 unresolved (all dynamic route args, not Phaser leaks). **Audited 2026-09-18:** `pnpm run inventory:three-closure` → 352 candidates, 331 reachable, 1664 edges, 18 unresolved (all `string-route` non-literal sceneKey/targetKey — expected dynamic dispatch, zero Phaser leaks).
- [~] **8.3 — PRE-CLEANUP EVIDENCE INVALIDATED; RE-RUN REQUIRED ON 2026-09-22+ HEAD. — Machine-readable manifest.** **Source symbol:** 100 inventory rows and tier partition. **Migration:** M1. **Allowed:** `docs/three-migration-manifest.json`, schema/validator. **Forbidden:** markdown-only owner/disposition claims; emitting a second tier vocabulary. **Known audit prerequisite (re-measured by PLAN 2026-09-17; the earlier parse defects are FIXED and must not be re-cited as open):** `parseTierMap()` (`scripts/generate-three-import-closure.mjs:107-125`) now scans per `### ...` heading — it no longer matches arbitrary 4-column rows — and normalizes every label to `Tier N (Name)` / `Adapters (...)`: measured **352/352 labels conform, 0 bare `Tier N`**, and **100/100 inventory-member rows match their inventory section with 0 mismatch**. **Audited 2026-09-18:** manifest validator passes; 100/100 inventory rows resolve to filesystem; 352/352 closure labels conform.
**8.3 precision note (PLAN, 2026-09-17 — advisory-driven).** The 2 rows under `### Structural Interface / Documentation-Only Adapters` carry the label `Adapters (Structural Interface / Documentation-Only Adapters)`, which is the intended second label family, **not** a mismatch: re-measured with verbatim `parseTierMap()` semantics against `docs/three-import-closure.json` → 100 inventory rows parsed, 100/100 present in the closure, **100/100 labels equal their inventory section, 0 mismatch**. The open decision is the other 252 rows: 8.3 must **either** declare the `inferTier()` categories a separate closure-only classification (so one artifact legitimately carries two vocabularies) **or** normalize them to the inventory tier names — it may not claim a single tier vocabulary while `Tier 3 (Core UI & Modals)`/`Tier 4 (Gameplay Mechanics…)`/`Tier 5 (Pure Three.js Domain)` reuse numbers that mean different things in the same file.

### Planning HUD zone rebalance — 2026-09-20

**Implemented + owner follow-ups:** Planning keeps the centered `three-planning-top-stack`. On wide layouts the Combat strip is one horizontal row in the order `Giao tranh | Cộng hưởng | Bắt đầu Chiến đấu | Hạ Cortisol`. On constrained widths, the heading and Start Combat stay together in the top strip while Synergy + Cortisol switch to compact icon copies in the right utility rail. Economy remains removed as a standalone titled panel. The Shop panel now owns one utility header row containing `Cửa hàng / Shop` at far left followed by exactly five controls: reroll, lock, XP, Sell and Unequip; Sell/Unequip keep their canonical drag-drop targets but read live DOM bounds from this row and remain visibly discoverable while disabled. The seven core management utilities (Tech, Inventory, Craft, Library, Achievements, History, Settings) stay icon-only at the far right edge and are shifted upward by about two button heights; hover/focus and 420ms long-press show a localized tooltip using the current i18n strings. The horizontal dark bar seen across the board was traced to the production medieval auto-surface classifier: class names containing `dock`/`panel` caused layout-only `three-planning-bottom-dock` / `three-planning-action-panel` wrappers to receive the generic 9px medieval panel frame/background. Those wrappers (plus the inner Shop card grid) now opt out with `data-medieval-surface="none"`, leaving only the intended Shop shell framed. The lower collapse affects Shop only; Combat and the desktop utility rail remain visible. The persistent Three camera joystick is vertically centered on the left edge.

**Tech-tree interaction follow-up:** `ThreePlanningTechRuntime` no longer calls Fit-to-view through every new `ResizeObserver` created by a node click/research re-render. Pan/zoom now survives node selection and research. The last selected focusable (unlocked/bought) tech node is persisted for the session/browser and becomes the default selected node next time the tree opens; initial view centers that node at a focused zoom instead of shrinking to the full graph. Explicit Fit / keyboard `0` still fits the full tree by user request.

### Settings tabbed responsive modal — 2026-09-20

**Implemented:** `ThreeSettingsModal` now uses six responsive tabs (Sound, Display, Gameplay, Shortcuts, Device, Data) instead of one vertically scrolling settings page. The settings shell and tab panels use `overflow:hidden`; shortcut bindings paginate four actions at a time so portrait/landscape mobile does not require a settings scrollbar. The active tab survives internal re-renders caused by quality, language, binding and pagination updates. Ordinary dismissible modals continue to use the global modal-dismiss normalizer. The only visible content is ASCII `-`, but the control uses the supplied medieval wooden plank asset so it matches the wooden settings controls instead of using an icon/round-danger asset.

### Medieval UI close-control robustness — 2026-09-20

**Close-control robustness:** static follow-up found several Three modules that build close buttons with `setFreeIconLabel(..., "close"/"✕", "")`, meaning the button can have no text label by the time the global UI classifier sees it. `installProductionUiAssets()` now detects the actual `[data-free-icon="close"]` atlas child, upgrades an already-classified `auto` button to `close` when the icon arrives after mount, and re-evaluates a parent button when MutationObserver sees a late-added icon node. This keeps Craft, Tech, unit-info and other compact close controls on `button-round-danger.png` instead of turning them into generic wood planks. Targeted source assertion added in `tests/three/threeProductionUiAssets.test.ts`; runtime/browser evidence remains pending.

- [x] **UI-PLANNING-POLISH-11 — move Cộng hưởng out of the top stats bar and remove the tree emoji from Tech Tree.** **Source symbol:** `ThreePlanningController.updateTopBarText()/updateActionButtons()`, shared i18n key `planning.ui.techTree`. **Migration method:** M4 Presentation adapter. **Allowed files:** Three Planning presentation, shared i18n labels, targeted tests. **Forbidden files/dependencies:** synergy calculations, tech-tree gameplay data, Phaser imports into `src/three/**`, edits to protected `old_src/`. **Targeted Vitest:** `pnpm exec vitest --run tests/three/threePlanningScene.test.ts`. **Runtime evidence:** pending Planning screenshot. **Done criterion:** top bar contains only the six round/gold/level/xp/deploy/hp stat chips; `.three-planning-synergy-button` lives in the lower Combat action group and opens the existing synergy modal; Tech Tree label contains no leading 🌳 in VI or EN and continues using the atlas-backed scroll icon.

## Combat red-circle readability verification plan — 2026-09-20

**Owner concern:** during Combat, a red circular marker around an animal is ambiguous because it looks like a persistent “currently attacking / being targeted” selector. Desired presentation is a red-tinted **ground/tile highlight**, matching the visual language of the existing purple turn-scan cell highlight.

**Static diagnosis:** the ambiguous marker comes from combat presentation VFX, not combat targeting authority. `ThreeCombatVfxRuntime.spawnImpact()` and `pulseLowHp()` both route through `spawnPulse()`. Historically that pulse used `THREE.RingGeometry` around the unit base, so impact/low-HP feedback visually resembled a selected/targeted unit. The canonical attacker/target choice remains owned by combat state/AI and must not be changed for this UI fix.

### Plan

- [x] **CRH-1 — Identify the red ring owner and keep gameplay untouched.**
  - **Source:** `src/three/ThreeCombatVfxRuntime.ts::spawnPulse()/spawnImpact()/pulseLowHp()`.
  - Confirm the ring is transient hit/low-HP presentation only; do not alter target selection, turn queue, damage, AI, or unit state.

- [x] **CRH-2 — Reuse the purple scan ground-highlight language.**
  - **Reference:** `src/three/ThreeRoundScene.ts::ensureTurnScanIndicator()`.
  - Replace unit-base `RingGeometry` with a horizontal `PlaneGeometry` fill plus `EdgesGeometry` / `LineSegments` border.
  - Keep the highlight attached to the board/ground position, slightly above terrain to avoid z-fighting.
  - Preserve damage-type color semantics; physical impact remains red/orange-red, magic remains blue, true damage remains pale gold.

- [x] **CRH-3 — Keep it transient and readable.**
  - Short scale pulse + opacity fade only; no persistent state marker.
  - Do not attach the highlight as a child of the animal model.
  - Ensure cleanup removes geometry/material/transient objects on animation completion and scene disposal.

- [x] **CRH-4 — Regression test the visual contract.**
  - **Test:** `tests/three/threeCombatVfx.test.ts`.
  - Assert combat pulse creates `combat-ground-impact-highlight` with `PlaneGeometry` + `LineSegments`.
  - Explicitly assert that this combat pulse contains no `RingGeometry`.

- [ ] **CRH-5 — Runtime visual verification before closure.**
  - Verify a basic physical attack: the struck cell flashes red on the ground and no red circle wraps the animal.
  - Verify low-HP pulse: same ground-highlight language, no persistent unit ring.
  - Verify magic/true-damage colors stay distinct.
  - Verify the purple turn-scan highlight remains unchanged and visually distinguishable.
  - Verify no highlight survives Combat → Planning, result, new round, or scene teardown.

**Current source status:** CRH-1 through CRH-4 are already present on `feature/threejs-migration` (the source-side correction landed before this owner follow-up was recorded). Only runtime/browser verification remains before marking this concern fully closed. Do not re-implement a second highlight system.


## Global Three font-size utility — 2026-09-20

**Status: SOURCE IMPLEMENTED / STATIC READBACK VERIFIED; runner/browser verification pending.**

- [x] **UI-TYPO-SIZE-14 — one semantic font-size authority for every production Three DOM scene.**
  - **Source:** `src/three/ThreeUiTypography.ts`, `ThreeProductionUiAssets.installProductionUiAssets()`, `ThreeMedievalButton`, late authority in `src/styles.css`.
  - **Roles/tokens:** `display`, `title`, `action`, `body`, `meta`, `caption`; each role resolves through a shared responsive `clamp(...)` CSS custom property rather than per-scene fixed px.
  - **Coverage:** `ThreeApp` installs production UI assets once on the application container. Existing DOM and every later scene/modal node observed by the shared `MutationObserver` receive semantic font roles, so Main Menu, Planning, Combat, Library, Settings, Augment, Fortress, Result and future Three DOM surfaces share one authority.
  - **Legacy containment:** old inline `fontSize` declarations may remain in scene modules for now, but production role styling is applied after them and the final CSS role selectors are deliberately late + `!important`. Equivalent actions therefore resolve to the same `action` token. Main Menu's explicit compact/normal 14px/18px fork was removed.
  - **Exceptions:** emoji/icon/ornament nodes keep their own pictogram size; they are not forced into body/action typography.
  - **Targeted regressions:** `tests/three/threeProductionUiAssets.test.ts`, `threeMedievalButton.test.ts`, and `threeMainMenuScene.test.ts` pin shared role assignment, late-created scene coverage, icon-size preservation, and equal Continue/Debug action sizes.
  - **Runtime evidence required:** refreshed Main Menu + Planning + Combat + one modal screenshot at desktop and mobile widths, confirming equivalent controls no longer drift in font size.


### Owner reopen — combat ring still visible (2026-09-20)

The owner correctly reopened this item after the first source pass: converting `spawnPulse()` from `RingGeometry` to a plane was not sufficient. Static re-audit found two remaining presentation problems: attack/skill motion still created impact feedback before the canonical damage outcome was known, and the post-skill path attached a persistent `aura_ring` directly to the acting animal.

- [x] **CRH-R1 — canonical hit event is the only target-highlight authority.** `ThreeRoundScene.onCombatDelta` now flashes the defender's actual `app.board.tiles[row][col]` only when `outcome === "hit"`. Misses never create the target highlight.
- [x] **CRH-R2 — copy the purple scan footprint exactly.** `ThreeCombatVfxRuntime.flashGroundTile()` uses `PlaneGeometry(TILE_SIZE * 0.96)` + `EdgesGeometry`, horizontal orientation, +0.27 terrain offset, pulse/fade, and a true red physical palette (`0xff4d4d` fill / `0xff1f1f` border).
- [x] **CRH-R3 — remove premature/model-relative hit markers.** Basic attack, skill, beam/projectile/contact motion no longer spawn their own impact marker; projectile completion no longer invents one before outcome authority.
- [x] **CRH-R4 — remove actor-attached combat aura ring.** The `ThreeRoundScene` SKILL completion path no longer creates `type: "aura_ring"` or retains a `starVisuals` action-marker map on combat actors.
- [x] **CRH-R5 — remove duplicate low-HP pulse from live hit flow.** Low HP remains readable from the HP/status HUD; the live `onCombatDelta` path no longer draws an additional marker around/under the unit.
- [x] **CRH-R6 — regression coverage.** VFX tests pin full-tile size, red fill/border, +0.27 scan height, no `RingGeometry`, and no marker before confirmed hit. Combat-scene tests pin exact board-cell snapping and reject skill-added `RingGeometry` on the acting animal.
- [ ] **CRH-R7 — browser runtime evidence.** Refresh deployed branch and visually confirm physical hits show a red square/tile flash while the purple turn scan remains purple and no action ring surrounds attacker/defender. Connector-only work must not claim this runtime observation.


### Owner follow-up — remove all circular unit markers + slow attack preview pulse (2026-09-20)

Owner screenshot still showed a red circular marker around a unit after the first combat-hit fix. Static re-audit found additional circular unit-facing geometry outside `ThreeCombatVfxRuntime`: avatar star layers, star/sigil factory layers, water puddles, poly-builder halo/aura parts, star torus ornaments, and the pooled/fallback pulse geometry.

- [x] **CIRCLE-1 — no circular unit indicator geometry in live Three surfaces.** `ThreeUnitAvatarRenderer`, `ThreeStarVisualEffectFactory`, `ThreeUnitModelFactory`, `ThreeUnitPolyBuilder`, `ThreeUnitStarVisuals`, `ThreeFxPool`, and the Three fallback in `ThreeVfx` no longer instantiate `RingGeometry`, `CircleGeometry`, or `TorusGeometry` for unit-facing aura/puddle/pulse decoration. Circular authored slots are rendered as faceted diamonds/diamond quads instead of target-like rings.
- [x] **CIRCLE-2 — combat hit readability stays tile-based.** Confirmed hit feedback remains the square/edge board-cell highlight owned by canonical `onCombatDelta`; no attacker/defender ring is reintroduced.
- [x] **PREVIEW-1 — direct target preview.** The direct/primary target cell is always included and continuously slow-pulses red (`0xff3b30`) on the board.
- [x] **PREVIEW-2 — indirect skill preview.** Every non-primary cell from `impact.cells` or `impact.skillCells` is deduped and continuously slow-pulses cyan (`0x6fd6ff`). One full pulse cycle is ~2.4 seconds.
- [x] **PREVIEW-3 — no circular preview badge chrome.** Planning preview badges use a small square radius instead of `border-radius: 50%`.
- [x] **Regression source coverage.** Planning tests pin red direct vs cyan indirect intensity changes; star VFX tests reject `RingGeometry`; live-model tests reject `RingGeometry` / `CircleGeometry` / `TorusGeometry` on representative 3★ and aquatic roots.
- [ ] **Runtime evidence.** Browser refresh still required to visually confirm no red/blue circles remain and that direct/indirect preview tiles breathe slowly at the intended cadence.


### Owner follow-up — drag into Shop panel + remove middle horizontal bar (2026-09-20)

- [x] **DRAG-SHOP-1 — browser pointer continuity over DOM Shop overlay.** `ThreeApp.createThreeInputBridge()` captures the active pointer on canvas at `pointerdown` and releases it at `pointerup/pointercancel`, so a drag that starts on the Three canvas keeps receiving move/release events after crossing into DOM Shop controls.
- [x] **DRAG-SHOP-2 — Shop drop targets beat board cells behind the overlay.** `ThreePlanningDragRuntime.getPlanningDropTarget()` now resolves Sell / Unequip before bench/board geometry, preventing a projected board cell under the Shop panel from stealing the drop.
- [x] **DRAG-SHOP-3 — dragged avatar stays visually above Shop sheets.** Floating drag cursor z-index is above the mobile Shop sheet/backdrop while retaining `pointer-events:none`.
- [x] **UI-BAR-1 — remove horizontal lifetime meter from Planning battlefield.** Planning log remains an auto-hiding toast but is taken out of the root flex flow and pinned top-right; `.three-planning-log-progress(-fill)` is no longer created and is CSS-suppressed for stale markup.
- [x] **Regression coverage.** `threeApp.test.ts` pins pointer capture/release; `threePlanningDragRuntime.test.js` pins Sell/Unequip precedence over overlapping board cells; `threePlanningScene.test.ts` pins drag cursor z-index and absence of the log progress bar.
- [ ] **Runtime evidence.** Refresh deployed branch and verify dragging a board/bench unit over the Shop panel keeps following the pointer and can drop onto Sell/Unequip, with no horizontal bar appearing mid-screen.
